    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. RV 29.66%→0.03% (↓99.9%), ET 8.18%→0% (↓100%). R-F1 7.74%→8.23% (+0.49pp), E-F1 58.15%→59.37% (+1.22pp). F1 method: all_set (OOV predictions included in denominator). 之前 recognized_set 方法 (R-F1=9.33%→8.17%, -1.16pp) 是错误的——它过滤了 unconstrained 的 5189 个 OOV predictions，人为抬高 precision。Grammar-only DR 上升(18.46→26.17%)是审稿必问点：grammar 强制 valid relation name 后 entity type 与新 relation 的 domain/range 不匹配加剧，恰好证明 semantic 层必要性。\\\", \\\"completed_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-14T02:55:47.271Z\\\", \\\"extra\\\": {\\\"previous_failure\\\": {\\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"failed\\\", \\\"notes\\\": \\\"修复后重启 (PID 157042, tmux: phase2_docred_full_s1, cuda:1)。根因: TypeConstraintProcessor._apply_type_mask 在 allowed_types {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"LogitsProcessor for runtime domain/range type constraint enforcement.\\n\\nLayered on top of XGrammar's structural constraints:\\n- XGrammar (Layer 1): Guarantees valid JSON, entity/relation type strings from enum\\n- TypeConstraintProcessor (Layer 2): Guarantees domain/range consistency\\n\\nAt each generation step:\\n1. Detect current JSON field position (entity type? relation head_type? tail_type?)\\n2. If at entity type position -> allow all entity type first tokens\\n3. If at head_type position -> mask to types valid for the current relation\\n4. If at tail_type position -> mask to types valid for (relation_type, head_type) pair\\n5. Apply mask: disallowed type tokens -> logits = -inf\\n\\nComposition:\\n    grammar_processor = xgr.contrib.hf.LogitsProcessor(compiled_grammar)\\n    type_processor = TypeConstraintProcessor(spec, tokenizer)\\n    model.generate(logits_processor=[grammar_processor, type_processor])\\n\\nFramework: HuggingFace transformers (API: __call__(input_ids, scores) -> scores)\\nDepends on: ontology_compiler.ConstraintSpec (type->token mappings + relation constraints)\\n\\nVerified first-token IDs (Qwen3-8B tokenizer, context-independent):\\n  SciERC:\\n    Task=6262, Method=3523, Material=13415, Metric=54310,\\n    OtherScientificTerm=11409, Generic=19964\\n  DocRED:\\n    PER=9654, ORG=44927, LOC=12052, TIME=18129, NUM=17488, MISC=44\\nAll first tokens are unique within each dataset -> simple first-token masking\\nis sufficient, no trie-based multi-token tracking needed.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport re\\nimport logging\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\\nfrom enum import Enum, auto\\nfrom typing import Optional\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import LogitsProcessor, PreTrainedTokenizerBase\\n\\nfrom .ontology_compiler import ConstraintSpec\\n\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\nclass FieldPosition(Enum):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Current position in the JSON output structure.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    UNKNOWN = auto()\\n    ENTITY_TYPE = auto()        # Inside entity's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TYPE = auto()      # Inside relation's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_HEAD_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\"head_type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TAIL_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\"tail_type\\\" field value\\n    FREE_TEXT = auto()           # Entity text, relation head/tail text, etc.\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass EntityRegistry:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Runtime registry of generated entities and their types.\\n\\n    Tracks text->type mappings from the entities list so that when a relation\\n    references an entity by text, we know its type for domain/range checking.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    entities: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\\n\\n    def register(self, text: str, entity_type: str):\\n        self.entities[text] = entity_type\\n\\n    def get_type(self, text: str) -> Optional[str]:\\n        return self.entities.get(text)\\n\\n    def clear(self):\\n        self.entities.clear()\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass GenerationState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Tracks the state of JSON generation for constraint application.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    position: FieldPosition = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n    current_relation_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_tail_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    entity_registry: EntityRegistry = field(default_factory=EntityRegistry)\\n    in_entities_section: bool = True\\n\\n    def reset(self):\\n        self.position = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n        self.current_relation_type = None\\n        self.current_head_type = None\\n        self.current_head_text = None\\n        self.current_tail_text = None\\n        self.entity_registry.clear()\\n        self.in_entities_section = True\\n\\n\\nclass PositionDetector:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Detects current field position by parsing generated text.\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.53}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have more context. W1 is about CF (Cross-Field consistency) violation explosion — when OntoCoDec applies constraints, CF violations skyrocket from 0.08% to 9.90%/12.02%. The "registry-consistent fallback" idea is to implement a fallback mechanism in `_apply_type_mask()` so that when the semantic constraint would cause a CF inconsistency, it falls back to a less restrictive constraint.

The worker `worker_code_w1_cf_registry_consistent_fallback` was supposed to implement this. Let me check:


I found a dry run result in `test_cf_fallback_dryrun.jsonl` (20KB with 10 samples from SciERC), but `run_constrained_extraction.py` hasn't been modified since May 14. I need to look at `compiler/type_mask_processor.py` where `_apply_type_mask()` actually lives to see if the fallback logic was added there.
```

</details>

Now I have a clear picture of the CF fallback implementation. Let me analyze it:

**What was implemented:**

In `_mask_head_type()` and `_mask_tail_type()`, the logic now:
1. Looks up the entity's registered type from the entity registry
2. If the registered type conflicts with the domain/range constraint for the current relation, it forces the registered type anyway (rather than applying the D/R mask that would produce a type mismatch)
3. This prevents CF violations by ensuring head_type/tail_type in relations match the entity's type from the entity list

**The logic is sound in principle:** When an entity "X" is declared as type "Task" in the entities list, and a relation references "X" as head, the head_type field should be "Task" regardless of D/R constraints. 

Forcing the registered type prevents the CF inconsistency where entity list says "Task" but relation says "Method". Now I need to verify a few things: whether the dry run output was actually generated with the new fallback code, whether this approach intentionally trades D/R violations for CF consistency, and what the actual performance metrics show for the Qwen DocRED full results.

Looking at the fallback rates, Qwen's full_s1 mode shows a significant 10.21% fallback rate across 1770 triples, while SciERC is much lower at 0.93%, which makes sense given its looser schema constraints. I should check the dry run output to confirm CF violations actually dropped, and I'm realizing the two currently running experiments are max_tokens ablations rather than the W1 CF fallback ablation I was tracking—that one hasn't started yet according to the roadmap. Let me also verify the GPU utilization for the active experiments.

The code implementation itself looks sound—the fallback mechanism correctly prioritizes registry consistency over D/R compliance by forcing registered types when conflicts arise, with proper logging. The dry run only covered about 10 SciERC samples though, so it's not enough for quantitative validation. The estimation analysis gave me the fallback rates, but I'm still missing the full ablation experiment to complete W1.

I need to check the GPU availability and review the decisions file to understand the current approach direction.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"LogitsProcessor for runtime domain/range type constraint enforcement.\\n\\nLayered on top of XGrammar's structural constraints:\\n- XGrammar (Layer 1): Guarantees valid JSON, entity/relation type strings from enum\\n- TypeConstraintProcessor (Layer 2): Guarantees domain/range consistency\\n\\nAt each generation step:\\n1. Detect current JSON field position (entity type? relation head_type? tail_type?)\\n2. If at entity type position -> allow all entity type first tokens\\n3. If at head_type position -> mask to types valid for the current relation\\n4. If at tail_type position -> mask to types valid for (relation_type, head_type) pair\\n5. Apply mask: disallowed type tokens -> logits = -inf\\n\\nComposition:\\n    grammar_processor = xgr.contrib.hf.LogitsProcessor(compiled_grammar)\\n    type_processor = TypeConstraintProcessor(spec, tokenizer)\\n    model.generate(logits_processor=[grammar_processor, type_processor])\\n\\nFramework: HuggingFace transformers (API: __call__(input_ids, scores) -> scores)\\nDepends on: ontology_compiler.ConstraintSpec (type->token mappings + relation constraints)\\n\\nVerified first-token IDs (Qwen3-8B tokenizer, context-independent):\\n  SciERC:\\n    Task=6262, Method=3523, Material=13415, Metric=54310,\\n    OtherScientificTerm=11409, Generic=19964\\n  DocRED:\\n    PER=9654, ORG=44927, LOC=12052, TIME=18129, NUM=17488, MISC=44\\nAll first tokens are unique within each dataset -> simple first-token masking\\nis sufficient, no trie-based multi-token tracking needed.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport re\\nimport logging\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\\nfrom enum import Enum, auto\\nfrom typing import Optional\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import LogitsProcessor, PreTrainedTokenizerBase\\n\\nfrom .ontology_compiler import ConstraintSpec\\n\\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\\n\\n\\nclass FieldPosition(Enum):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Current position in the JSON output structure.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    UNKNOWN = auto()\\n    ENTITY_TYPE = auto()        # Inside entity's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TYPE = auto()      # Inside relation's \\\"type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_HEAD_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\"head_type\\\" field value\\n    RELATION_TAIL_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\"tail_type\\\" field value\\n    FREE_TEXT = auto()           # Entity text, relation head/tail text, etc.\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass EntityRegistry:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Runtime registry of generated entities and their types.\\n\\n    Tracks text->type mappings from the entities list so that when a relation\\n    references an entity by text, we know its type for domain/range checking.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    entities: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\\n\\n    def register(self, text: str, entity_type: str):\\n        self.entities[text] = entity_type\\n\\n    def get_type(self, text: str) -> Optional[str]:\\n        return self.entities.get(text)\\n\\n    def clear(self):\\n        self.entities.clear()\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass GenerationState:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Tracks the state of JSON generation for constraint application.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    position: FieldPosition = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n    current_relation_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_type: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_head_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    current_tail_text: Optional[str] = None\\n    entity_registry: EntityRegistry = field(default_factory=EntityRegistry)\\n    in_entities_section: bool = True\\n\\n    def reset(self):\\n        self.position = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\n        self.current_relation_type = None\\n        self.current_head_type = None\\n        self.current_head_text = None\\n        self.current_tail_text = None\\n        self.entity_registry.clear()\\n        self.in_entities_section = True\\n\\n\\nclass PositionDetector:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Detects current field position by parsing generated text.\\n\\n    Regex-based parsing of JSON text generated so far. Called at every\\n    decoding step, so only inspects the tail of the text.\\n\\n    Future optimization: use XGrammar's GrammarMatcher state for O(1) per step\\n    instead of O(n) text parsing.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    _RE_TAIL_TYPE = re.compile(r'\\\"tail_type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"[^\\\"]*$')\\n    _RE_HEAD_TYPE = re.compile(r'\\\"head_type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"[^\\\"]*$')\\n    _RE_TYPE_FIELD = re.compile(r'\\\"type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"[^\\\"]*$')\\n\\n    _RE_ENTITY = re.compile(r'\\\"text\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"\\\\s*,\\\\s*\\\"type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"')\\n\\n    _RE_REL_TYPE_VAL = re.compile(r'\\\"type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"')\\n    _RE_HEAD_TYPE_VAL = re.compile(r'\\\"head_type\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"')\\n    _RE_HEAD_TEXT_VAL = re.compile(r'\\\"head\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"')\\n    _RE_TAIL_TEXT_VAL = re.compile(r'\\\"tail\\\"\\\\s*:\\\\s*\\\"([^\\\"]+)\\\"')\\n    _RE_REL_START = re.compile(r'\\\\{\\\\s*\\\"head\\\"')\\n\\n    @classmethod\\n    def detect(cls, generated_text: str, state: GenerationState) -> FieldPosition:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Determine which JSON field is currently being generated.\\n\\n        Mutates state in place: updates entity registry, current relation type,\\n        current head type, etc.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if '\\\"relations\\\"' in generated_text:\\n            state.in_entities_section = False\\n\\n        for match in cls._RE_ENTITY.finditer(generated_text):\\n            state.entity_registry.register(match.group(1), match.group(2))\\n\\n        # Find the last relation object being generated\\n        last_rel_start = -1\\n        for m in cls._RE_REL_START.finditer(generated_text):\\n            last_rel_start = m.start()\\n\\n        if last_rel_start >= 0:\\n            partial_rel = generated_text[last_rel_start:]\\n\\n            m = cls._RE_REL_TYPE_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\n            if m:\\n                state.current_relation_type = m.group(1)\\n\\n            m = cls._RE_HEAD_TYPE_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\n            if m:\\n                state.current_head_type = m.group(1)\\n\\n            m = cls._RE_HEAD_TEXT_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\n            if m:\\n                state.current_head_text = m.group(1)\\n\\n            m = cls._RE_TAIL_TEXT_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\n            if m:\\n                state.current_tail_text = m.group(1)\\n\\n        # Determine position from last 100 chars\\n        tail = generated_text[-100:]\\n\\n        if cls._RE_TAIL_TYPE.search(tail):\\n            return FieldPosition.RELATION_TAIL_TYPE\\n\\n        if cls._RE_HEAD_TYPE.search(tail):\\n            return FieldPosition.RELATION_HEAD_TYPE\\n\\n        if cls._RE_TYPE_FIELD.search(tail):\\n            if state.in_entities_section:\\n                return FieldPosition.ENTITY_TYPE\\n            else:\\n                return FieldPosition.RELATION_TYPE\\n\\n        return FieldPosition.FREE_TEXT\\n\\n\\nclass TypeConstraintProcessor(LogitsProcessor):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Enforces domain/range type constraints during autoregressive generation.\\n\\n    Constraint logic:\\n    - ENTITY_TYPE: Allow all entity type first tokens (defense-in-depth with XGrammar)\\n    - RELATION_HEAD_TYPE: Given relation R, allow types T where exists T' s.t. (T,T') in R.valid_pairs\\n    - RELATION_TAIL_TYPE: Given relation R and head H, allow types T where (H,T) in R.valid_pairs\\n      <-- THIS IS THE KEY CONSTRAINT\\n\\n    Usage:\\n        spec, compiled_grammar = compile_ontology(\\\"ontology_constraints.json\\\", tokenizer)\\n        grammar_proc = xgr.contrib.hf.LogitsProcessor(compiled_grammar)\\n        type_proc = TypeConstraintProcessor(spec, tokenizer, prompt_length=len(input_ids[0]))\\n        output = model.generate(**inputs, logits_processor=[grammar_proc, type_proc])\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(\\n        self,\\n        spec: ConstraintSpec,\\n        tokenizer: PreTrainedTokenizerBase,\\n        prompt_length: int = 0,\\n    ):\\n        self.spec = spec\\n        self.tokenizer = tokenizer\\n        self.prompt_length = prompt_length\\n        self.state = GenerationState()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

Inside entity's \\\\\\\"type\\\\\\\" field value\\\\n    RELATION_TYPE = auto()      # Inside relation's \\\\\\\"type\\\\\\\" field value\\\\n    RELATION_HEAD_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\\\\\"head_type\\\\\\\" field value\\\\n    RELATION_TAIL_TYPE = auto()  # Inside relation's \\\\\\\"tail_type\\\\\\\" field value\\\\n    FREE_TEXT = auto()           # Entity text, relation head/tail text, etc.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n@dataclass\\\\nclass EntityRegistry:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Runtime registry of generated entities and their types.\\\\n\\\\n    Tracks text->type mappings from the entities list so that when a relation\\\\n    references an entity by text, we know its type for domain/range checking.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    entities: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\\\\n\\\\n    def register(self, text: str, entity_type: str):\\\\n        self.entities[text] = entity_type\\\\n\\\\n    def get_type(self, text: str) -> Optional[str]:\\\\n        return self.entities.get(text)\\\\n\\\\n    def clear(self):\\\\n        self.entities.clear()\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n@dataclass\\\\nclass GenerationState:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Tracks the state of JSON generation for constraint application.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    position: FieldPosition = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\\\n    current_relation_type: Optional[str] = None\\\\n    current_head_type: Optional[str] = None\\\\n    current_head_text: Optional[str] = None\\\\n    current_tail_text: Optional[str] = None\\\\n    entity_registry: EntityRegistry = field(default_factory=EntityRegistry)\\\\n    in_entities_section: bool = True\\\\n\\\\n    def reset(self):\\\\n        self.position = FieldPosition.UNKNOWN\\\\n        self.current_relation_type = None\\\\n        self.current_head_type = None\\\\n        self.current_head_text = None\\\\n        self.current_tail_text = None\\\\n        self.entity_registry.clear()\\\\n        self.in_entities_section = True\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass PositionDetector:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Detects current field position by parsing generated text.\\\\n\\\\n    Regex-based parsing of JSON text generated so far. Find the last relation object being generated\\\\n        last_rel_start = -1\\\\n        for m in cls._RE_REL_START.finditer(generated_text):\\\\n            last_rel_start = m.start()\\\\n\\\\n        if last_rel_start >= 0:\\\\n            partial_rel = generated_text[last_rel_start:]\\\\n\\\\n            m = cls._RE_REL_TYPE_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\\\n            if m:\\\\n                state.current_relation_type = m.group(1)\\\\n\\\\n            m = cls._RE_HEAD_TYPE_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\\\n            if m:\\\\n                state.current_head_type = m.group(1)\\\\n\\\\n            m = cls._RE_HEAD_TEXT_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\\\n            if m:\\\\n                state.current_head_text = m.group(1)\\\\n\\\\n            m = cls._RE_TAIL_TEXT_VAL.search(partial_rel)\\\\n            if m:\\\\n                state.current_tail_text = m.group(1)\\\\n\\\\n        # <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a complete picture. Let me analyze the W1 CF fallback implementation:

**What was done (code):**

The worker modified `compiler/type_mask_processor.py` to add "registry-consistent fallback" logic in `_mask_head_type()` and `_mask_tail_type()`:

- When an entity's registered type (from the entity list) conflicts with D/R constraints for the current relation, the fallback forces the registered type instead of applying the D/R mask.
- This trades DR violations for CF consistency: "registry consistency > DR compliance"
- Counter `_cf_fallback_count` tracks how often this happens.


- Proper logging was added to track these events.

**What was done (analysis):**

I ran `estimate_fallback_rate.py` to measure how often the fallback triggers on existing outputs, finding that Qwen DocRED hits it 10.21% of the time (1770/17341 triples) while Qwen SciERC is much lower at 0.93% (16/1729). I also created a small dryrun test with about 10 SciERC samples to validate the new code.

**Issues to check:**

The implementation logic checks out—when entity "X" is typed "Task" in the registry, the head and tail type masking should respect that registered type even if it violates D/R constraints.

This creates a trade-off: the new approach prevents CF inconsistencies by prioritizing registry consistency over domain/range compliance, which means some relations will become D/R invalid. Since the old code was already generating D/R violations through fallback-to-grammar, the real question is whether this CF-consistent approach increases the overall D/R violation count. So the key point is: When the entity is registered as type T but T ∉ dom(r), the old behavior was to apply the D/R mask which would force a different type → creating a CF violation. The new behavior forces T → this prevents the CF violation but the relation will have a DR violation.

**But the relation already had a DR violation in the "unconstrained" sense** — the model picked entity type T and relation R where T ∉ dom(R). The question is whether the constraint decoder should override entity type assignments to match relation constraints (old behavior, causes CF) or maintain entity type consistency and accept the D/R violation (new behavior, prevents CF).

The new approach is defensible: entity type assignment is more "primary" — the model committed to that type first, and the relation's head_type/tail_type should be consistent with the entity list. A DR violation is already a semantic issue, but at least the output is internally consistent.

I still need to run the full ablation experiment — the roadmap calls for about 12 GPU-hours once P1 finishes, but the two GPUs are currently occupied with max_tokens experiments. The dry run on just ~10 SciERC samples isn't enough to validate this quantitatively. I should also verify GPU health directly and check whether the running experiments are actually using the correct devices, since the registry shows some mismatches between the configured GPU and the command-line device arguments. I need to verify the GPU allocation by checking nvidia-smi to see which physical GPUs are actually being used, and then cross-reference that with the project's configuration to understand how the CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES mapping works.
```

```text
Let me verify the GPU assignment. The registry shows:
- `llama_docred_unconstrained_mt6144`: pid 246947, gpu cuda:0
- `llama_docred_full_s1_mt6144`: pid 247487, gpu cuda:1

nvidia-smi shows:
- PID 246952 on GPU-0ce19412... (need to match GPU UUID to index)
- PID 247492 on GPU-342cbca6...

The PIDs are close but not exact matches (246947 vs 246952, 247487 vs 247492). 

The PID discrepancies likely stem from child processes spawned by the main ones. Both GPUs are running hot at 89% and 83% utilization with ~18GB each on these high-end RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell cards. I need to cross-reference the GPU UUIDs to their indices and verify the project's gpu_ids configuration to ensure there's no allocation violation.
```

self._all_type_first_tokens = self._build_first_token_map()\\n        self._grammar_first_skip_count = 0\\n        self._cf_fallback_count = 0\\n\\n    def _build_first_token_map(self) -> tuple[dict[str, int], set[int]]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Build entity_type -> first_token_id mapping.\\n\\n        Uses pre-computed token IDs from ConstraintSpec.entity_type_first_token\\n        (set by OntologyCompiler.compile_type_constraints) if available.\\n        Falls back to ConstraintSpec.entity_type_token_ids, then tokenizer.encode().\\n\\n        All first tokens verified unique for Qwen3-8B on both SciERC and DocRED,\\n        so simple first-token masking is sufficient (no trie needed).\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        type_first_token: dict[str, int] = {}\\n        all_first_tokens: set[int] = set()\\n\\n        for etype in self.spec.entity_types:\\n            # Priority: pre-compiled first token > full token list > tokenizer\\n            if etype in self.spec.entity_type_first_token:\\n                first_id = self.spec.entity_type_first_token[etype]\\n            else:\\n                token_ids = self.spec.entity_type_token_ids.get(etype)\\n                if token_ids is None:\\n                    token_ids = self.tokenizer.encode(etype, add_special_tokens=False)\\n                first_id = token_ids[0]\\n\\n            type_first_token[etype] = first_id\\n            all_first_tokens.add(first_id)\\n\\n        return type_first_token, all_first_tokens\\n\\n    def _detect_position(self, generated_text: str) -> FieldPosition:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Detect current token's semantic position in the JSON structure.\\n\\n        Delegates to PositionDetector.detect() which parses the generated text\\n        and updates self.state (relation type, head type, entity registry, etc.).\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self.detector.detect(generated_text, self.state)\\n\\n    def __call__(\\n        self,\\n        input_ids: torch.LongTensor,\\n        scores: torch.FloatTensor,\\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply domain/range constraints to logits.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            input_ids: [batch_size, seq_len] -- full sequence including prompt\\n            scores: [batch_size, vocab_size] -- logits for next token\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            Modified scores with disallowed type tokens set to -inf\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        batch_size = input_ids.shape[0]\\n\\n        for batch_idx in range(batch_size):\\n            generated_ids = input_ids[batch_idx, self.prompt_length:].tolist()\\n            generated_text = self.tokenizer.decode(\\n                generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True\\n            )\\n\\n            position = self._detect_position(generated_text)\\n\\n            if position == FieldPosition.ENTITY_TYPE:\\n                scores[batch_idx] = self._mask_entity_type(scores[batch_idx])\\n\\n            elif position == FieldPosition.RELATION_HEAD_TYPE:\\n                scores[batch_idx] = self._mask_head_type(scores[batch_idx])\\n\\n            elif position == FieldPosition.RELATION_TAIL_TYPE:\\n                scores[batch_idx] = self._mask_tail_type(scores[batch_idx])\\n\\n        return scores\\n\\n    def _mask_entity_type(self, logits: torch.FloatTensor) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"At entity type position, allow only valid entity type first tokens.\\n\\n        XGrammar's enum constraint already restricts to valid types, but this\\n        provides defense-in-depth by masking at the logit level.\\n        All entity types are allowed here (no domain/range filtering at this stage).\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self._apply_type_mask(logits, set(self.spec.entity_types))\\n\\n    def _mask_head_type(self, logits: torch.FloatTensor) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Mask entity types that cannot be head_type for the current relation.\\n\\n        Strategy: intersect entity registry type with D/R-valid types.\\n        - If entity type known AND D/R-valid → force that single type\\n    - If entity type known but NOT D/R-valid → force registered type\\n      (W1: re\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 3422 字符] ...\\n\\n   return logits\\n\\n    # Grammar-First Invariant: semantic mask ⊆ grammar allowed set\\n    #\\n    # When XGrammar marks grammar-invalid tokens with finite negative values\\n    # (e.g., -1e10) rather than -inf, and semantic masking sets all grammar-valid\\n    # type tokens to -inf (when allowed_types ∩ grammar-valid = ∅), argmax\\n    # selects a grammar-invalid token → XGrammar accept_token assertion failure.\\n    #\\n    # Resolution: Before applying semantic mask, verify at least one semantically-\\n    # allowed type has a non-grammar-masked logit (> _GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD).\\n    # If no valid intersection exists, skip semantic masking entirely and let\\n    # grammar control token selection.\\n    #\\n    # This invariant is the direct evidence for the \\\"enabling dependency\\\" claim:\\n    # grammar layer is a prerequisite for semantic layer correctness, not just\\n    # structural validity. Triggered on DocRED (avg 5.68 valid pairs, tight\\n    # constraints) but not SciERC (avg 24 valid pairs, loose constraints).\\n    _GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD = -1e8\\n\\n    def _apply_type_mask(\\n        self,\\n        logits: torch.FloatTensor,\\n        allowed_types: set[str],\\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Set logits of disallowed type first-tokens to -inf.\\n\\n        Safety: if all semantically-allowed types are already grammar-masked,\\n        skip semantic masking to prevent all-inf state that causes XGrammar\\n        assertion failure (grammar uses finite mask values < -inf).\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if not allowed_types:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        disallowed_first_tokens = [\\n            tid for etype, tid in self._type_first_token.items()\\n            if etype not in allowed_types\\n        ]\\n\\n        if not disallowed_first_tokens:\\n            return logits\\n\\n        # Verify at least one allowed type has a non-masked logit\\n        any_allowed_valid = any(\\n            logits[self._type_first_token[t]].item() > self._GRAMMAR_MASK_THRESHOLD\\n            for t in allowed_types\\n            if t in self._type_first_token\\n        )\\n        if not any_allowed_valid:\\n            self._grammar_first_skip_count += 1\\n            logger.warning(\\\"Grammar-first fallback triggered (skip_count=%d): semantic mask would eliminate all grammar-valid tokens\\\", self._grammar_first_skip_count)\\n            return logits\\n\\n        logits[disallowed_first_tokens] = float(\\\"-inf\\\")\\n\\n        return logits\\n\\n    def reset(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reset state between generate() calls.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if self._grammar_first_skip_count > 0:\\n            logger.info(\\\"Grammar-first fallback triggered %d times this generation\\\", self._grammar_first_skip_count)\\n        if self._cf_fallback_count > 0:\\n            logger.info(\\\"CF registry-consistent fallback triggered %d times this generation\\\", self._cf_fallback_count)\\n        self._grammar_first_skip_count = 0\\n        self._cf_fallback_count = 0\\n        self.state.reset()\\n\\n\\n# ── Integration Helper ───────────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\ndef build_constrained_processors(\\n    constraints_path: str,\\n    tokenizer: for LogitsProcessorList:\\n        [xgrammar_processor, type_constraint_processor]\\n\\n    Usage:\\n        processors = build_constrained_processors(\\n            \\\"ontology_constraints.json\\\", tokenizer, len(input_ids[0])\\n        )\\n        output = model.generate(**inputs, logits_processor=processors)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    import xgrammar as xgr\\n    from .ontology_compiler import compile_ontology\\n\\n    spec, compiled_grammar = compile_ontology(constraints_path, tokenizer)\\n\\n    processors = []\\n\\n    if compiled_grammar is not None:\\n        grammar_processor = xgr.contrib.hf.LogitsProcessor(compiled_grammar)\\n        processors.append(grammar_processor)\\n\\n    type_processor = TypeConstraintProcessor(spec, tokenizer, prompt_length)\\n Reviewing my findings so far: the CF fallback implementation appears sound, with the `_mask_head_type()` and `_mask_tail_type()` methods now correctly prioritizing entity registry consistency over domain/range constraints—if an entity is registered as a particular type, that type gets forced into the head and tail positions regardless of whether it's in the relation's domain or range.

GPU allocation looks healthy with good compute utilization (89%, 83%) and reasonable memory usage for inference. One thing I haven't fully analyzed yet is how the CF fallback interacts with the existing grammar-first fallback mechanism—the CF fallback forces a registered type, which then gets masked by _apply_type_mask, and that could potentially trigger the grammar-first fallback as a secondary effect.

Actually, tracing through the code more carefully, when _mask_head_type calls _apply_type_mask with a single forced type (whether from the registered CF type or the D/R constraint), the grammar-first check inside _apply_type_mask will validate that the forced type has at least one non-masked logit, so the interaction is implicitly handled rather than being a real conflict.

Now I'm checking whether run_constrained_extraction.py needs updates to activate this fallback, or if it's automatically available since the logic lives in type_mask_processor.py — looking at how TypeConstraintProcessor gets instantiated through build_constrained_processors(), the fallback should already be active.

This means the P1 experiments running now (llama_docred_*_mt6144, submitted after the May 15 code change) are already using the new CF fallback, even though they're primarily designed to test max_tokens ablation rather than CF behavior specifically.

For the actual CF ablation, I need to compare results with the fallback active against the existing baseline results that ran without it — but there's no configuration flag to toggle the fallback on and off since it's hardcoded into the code. So the ablation likely means running the full constrained extraction pipeline with the new code and measuring violation rates against the previous results.

Now I'm cross-checking the existing experimental results against what's documented in the ROADMAP. I need to verify these match what's actually in the registry for the phase 2 experiments.

The W1 CF fallback should reduce all of these violation rates, so the ablation study needs to clearly show the before-and-after impact. The worker has implemented the fallback code and done a minimal dry run, but the full ablation hasn't started yet.

Looking at potential implementation issues, I'm concerned about the entity text matching logic — it's doing exact string matching on entity text, so if a relation references an entity with slightly different wording, the lookup will fail and the fallback won't trigger properly.

The fallback only activates when the entity type exists in the recognized token set, which is correct behavior. Both head and tail branches have the fallback implemented, the counter and logging look good, and since there's no mode flag to toggle it on and off, I'll need to use existing results as the baseline for comparison.
